Show that $Z(t) = -W(t)$ is a Wiener process if $W(t)$ is.

Hint Are the increments of $Z(t)$ independent? How are they distributed? Are the paths of $Z(t)$ continuous? Is it true that $Z(0) = 0$ ?

# Exercise 6.34

Show that for any $0 \leq s < t$

$$
P \left\{W (t) \in A | W (s) \right\} = \int_ {A} p \left(t - s, W (s), y\right) d y.
$$

Hint Write the conditional probability as the conditional expectation of $1_{A}(W(t))$ given $W(s)$ . Compute the conditional expectation by transforming the integral of $1_{A}(W(t))$ over any event in the $\sigma$ -field generated by $W(s)$ . This can be done using the joint density of $W(s)$ and $W(t)$ . Refer to the chapter on conditional expectation if necessary.

# Exercise 6.35

Show that $e^{W(t)}e^{-\frac{t}{2}}$ is a martingale. (It is called the exponential martingale.)

Hint What is the expectation of $e^{W(t) - W(s)}$ for $s < t$ ? By independence it is equal to the conditional expectation of $e^{W(t) - W(s)}$ given $\mathcal{F}_s$ . This will give the martingale condition.

# Exercise 6.36

Compute $E(W(s)|W(t))$ for $0 \leq s < t$ .

Hint You want to find a Borel function $F$ such that $E(W(s)|W(t)) = F(W(t))$ , i.e.

$$
\int_ {\{W (t) \in A \}} W (s) d P = \int_ {\{W (t) \in A \}} F (W (t)) d P.
$$

Either side of this equality can be transformed using the joint density of $W(t)$ and $W(s)$ .

# 6.4 Solutions

# Solution 6.1

Suppose that $\eta$ is a random variable with exponential distribution of rate $\lambda$ . The distribution function of $\eta$ is

$$
F (t) = P \left\{\eta \leq t \right\} = 1 - P \left\{\eta > t \right\} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0 & \text {if} t <   0, \\ 1 - e ^ {- \lambda t} & \text {if} t \geq 0. \end{array} \right.
$$

Therefore $\eta$ has density

$$
f (t) = \frac {d}{d t} F (t) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0 & \quad \text { if } t <   0, \\ \lambda e ^ {- \lambda t} & \quad \text { if } t > 0. \end{array} \right.
$$

The distribution function $F(t)$ and density $f(t)$ are shown in Figure 6.6.

# Solution 6.2

Using the density $f(t) = \lambda e^{-\lambda t}$ found in Exercise 6.1 and integrating by parts,

![](images/6f383241ec563e2749359d0d5dfcee8ca02237c4809b65a13fd2f2116b785171.jpg)  
Figure 6.6. The distribution function $F(t)$ and density $f(t)$ of a random variable with exponential distribution of rate $\lambda = 2$

we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} E (\eta) = \int_ {- \infty} ^ {\infty} t f (t) d t = \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t \lambda e ^ {- \lambda t} d t = - \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t \frac {d}{d t} e ^ {- \lambda t} d t \\ = - t e ^ {- \lambda t} \left| _ {0} ^ {\infty} + \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} e ^ {- \lambda t} d t = 0 - \frac {1}{\lambda} e ^ {- \lambda t} \right| _ {0} ^ {\infty} = \frac {1}{\lambda}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In a similar way we compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} E (\eta^ {2}) = \int_ {- \infty} ^ {\infty} t ^ {2} f (t) d t = \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t ^ {2} \lambda e ^ {- \lambda t} d t = - \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t ^ {2} \frac {d}{d t} e ^ {- \lambda t} d t \\ = \left. - t ^ {2} e ^ {- \lambda t} \right| _ {0} ^ {\infty} + 2 \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t e ^ {- \lambda t} d t = 0 + \frac {2}{\lambda} \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} t f (t) d t = \frac {2}{\lambda^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

It follows that the variance is equal to

$$
\operatorname{var} (\eta) = E \left(\eta^ {2}\right) - (E (\eta)) ^ {2} = \frac {2}{\lambda^ {2}} - \frac {1}{\lambda^ {2}} = \frac {1}{\lambda^ {2}}.
$$

# Solution 6.3

By the definition of a random variable with exponential distribution

$$
P \left\{\eta > s + t \right\} = e ^ {- \lambda (s + t)} = e ^ {- \lambda s} e ^ {- \lambda t} = P \left\{\eta > s \right\} P \left\{\eta > t \right\}.
$$

# Solution 6.4

By the definition of conditional probability

$$
\begin{array}{l} P \left\{\eta > t + s | \eta > s \right\} = \frac {P \left\{\eta > t + s , \eta > s \right\}}{P \left\{\eta > s \right\}} \\ = \frac {P \left\{\eta > t + s \right\}}{P \left\{\eta > s \right\}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

since $\{\eta > t + s, \eta > s\} = \{\eta > t + s\}$ (because $\eta > s + t$ implies that $\eta > s$ ). Now it is clear that (6.1) is equivalent to (6.2).

# Solution 6.5

The exponential distribution is the only probability distribution satisfying the lack of memory property because the only non-negative non-increasing solutions of the functional equation

$$
g (t + s) = g (t) g (s)
$$

are of the form $g(t) = a^t$ for some $0 \leq a \leq 1$ .

To verify this observe that $[g(m / n)]^n = g(m) = [g(1)]^m$ for any integers $m$ and $n \neq 0$ . Let $a := g(1)$ . It follows that

$$
g (q) = a ^ {q} \quad \text {   for   any   } q \in \mathbb {Q}.
$$

Since $g$ is non-increasing, $0 \leq a \leq 1$ and

$$
a ^ {t} = \inf _ {t > q \in \mathbb {Q}} g (q) \geq g (t) \geq \sup _ {t <   q \in \mathbb {Q}} g (q) = a ^ {t},
$$

so indeed

$$
g (t) = a ^ {t} \quad \text {   for   any   } t \in \mathbb {R}.
$$

As a result, $P\{\eta > t\} = a^t$ for some $0 \leq a \leq 1$ . But the distribution function of a random variable cannot be constant, so $0 \neq a \neq 1$ . Hence $a = e^{-\lambda}$ for some $\lambda > 0$ , completing the argument.

# Solution 6.6

Since $N(t)$ has the Poisson distribution with parameter $\lambda t$ we have $E(N(t)) = \lambda t$ . Indeed

$$
\begin{array}{l} E (N (t)) = \sum_ {n = 0} ^ {\infty} n P \{N (t) = n \} = \sum_ {n = 0} ^ {\infty} n e ^ {- \lambda t} \frac {(\lambda t) ^ {n}}{n !} \\ = \lambda t e ^ {- \lambda t} \sum_ {n = 1} ^ {\infty} \frac {(\lambda t) ^ {n - 1}}{(n - 1) !} = \lambda t e ^ {- \lambda t} e ^ {\lambda t} = \lambda t. \\ \end{array}
$$

# Solution 6.7

Using the fact that $\eta_1, \eta_2, \ldots$ are independent and exponentially distributed, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} P \{N (s) = 1, N (t) = 2 \} = P \left\{\xi_ {1} \leq s <   \xi_ {2} \leq t <   \xi_ {3} \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {1} \leq s <   \eta_ {1} + \eta_ {2} \leq t <   \eta_ {1} + \eta_ {2} + \eta_ {3} \right\} \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {s} P \left\{s <   u + \eta_ {2} \leq t <   u + \eta_ {2} + \eta_ {3} \right\} \lambda e ^ {- \lambda u} d u \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {s} \left(\int_ {s - u} ^ {t - u} P \{t <   u + v + \eta_ {3} \} \lambda e ^ {- \lambda v} d v\right) \lambda e ^ {- \lambda u} d u \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \int_ {0} ^ {s} \left(\int_ {s - u} ^ {t - u} e ^ {- \lambda (t - u - v)} \lambda e ^ {- \lambda v} d v\right) \lambda e ^ {- \lambda u} d u \\ = \lambda^ {2} e ^ {- \lambda t} s (t - s). \\ \end{array}
$$

# Solution 6.8

Since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \xi_ {n} ^ {t} = \eta_ {1} ^ {t} + \dots + \eta_ {n} ^ {t} \\ = \xi_ {N (t) + 1} - t + \eta_ {N (t) + 2} + \dots + \eta_ {N (t) + n} \\ = \xi_ {N (t) + n} - t, \\ \end{array}
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} N ^ {t} (s) = \max \left\{n: \xi_ {n} ^ {t} \leq s \right\} \\ = \max \left\{n: \xi_ {N (t) + n} \leq t + s \right\} \\ = \max \left\{n: \xi_ {n} \leq t + s \right\} - N (t) \\ = N (t + s) - N (t). \\ \end{array}
$$

# Solution 6.9

It is easily verified that

$$
\{N (t) = n \} = \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\}
$$

$$
\left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s, N (t) = n \right\} = \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s + t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\}
$$

Since $\xi_{n},\eta_{n+1}$ are independent and $\eta_{n+1}$ satisfies the lack of memory property from Exercise 6.3,

$$
\begin{array}{l} P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s, N (t) = n \right\} = P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s + t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\} \\ = \int_ {- \infty} ^ {t} P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s + t - u \right\} P _ {\xi_ {n}} (d u) \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s \right\} \int_ {- \infty} ^ {t} P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > t - u \right\} P _ {\xi_ {n}} (d u) \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s \right\} P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s \right\} P \{N (t) = n \} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {1} > s \right\} P \{N (t) = n \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The last equality holds because $\eta_{n+1}$ has the same distribution as $\eta_1$ . Now divide both sides by $P\{N(t) = n\}$ to get

$$
P \{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s | N (t) = n \} = P \{\eta_ {1} > s \}
$$

for any $n = 0,1,2,\ldots$ . As a result,

$$
P \{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s | N (t) \} = P \{\eta_ {1} > s \}
$$

because $N(t)$ is a discrete random variable with values $0,1,2,\ldots$ .

# Solution 6.10

As in Solution 6.9,

$$
\{N (t) = n \} = \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\},
$$

$$
\left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, N (t) = n \right\} = \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s _ {1} + t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\}
$$

and, more generally,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, \eta_ {2} ^ {t} > s _ {2}, \dots , \eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k}, N (t) = n \right\} \\ = \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s _ {1} + t - \xi_ {n}, t \geq \xi_ {n} \right\} \cap \left\{\eta_ {n + 2} > s _ {2} \right\} \cap \dots \cap \left\{\eta_ {n + k} > s _ {k} \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since $\xi_{n},\eta_{n + 1},\ldots ,\eta_{n + k}$ are independent and $\eta_{n + 2},\dots ,\eta_{n + k}$ have the same distribution as $\eta_2,\dots ,\eta_k$ using Exercise 6.9 we find that

$$
\begin{array}{l} P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, \eta_ {2} ^ {t} > s _ {2}, \dots , \eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k}, N (t) = n \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {n + 1} > s _ {1} + t - \xi_ {n}, \xi_ {n} \leq t \right\} P \left\{\eta_ {n + 2} > s _ {2} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {n + k} > s _ {k} \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, N (t) = n \right\} P \left\{\eta_ {2} > s _ {2} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} > s _ {k} \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1} \mid N (t) = n \right\} P \left\{\eta_ {2} > s _ {2} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} > s _ {k} \right\} P \left\{N (t) = n \right\} \\ = P \left\{\eta_ {1} > s _ {1} \right\} P \left\{\eta_ {2} > s _ {2} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} > s _ {k} \right\} P \{N (t) = n \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

As in Solution 6.9, this implies the desired equality.

# Solution 6.11

Take the expectation on both sides of the equality in Exercise 6.10 to find that

$$
P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, \dots , \eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k} \right\} = P \left\{\eta_ {1} > s _ {1} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} > s _ {k} \right\}.
$$

If all the numbers $s_n$ except perhaps one are zero, it follows that

$$
P \{\eta_ {n} ^ {t} > s _ {n} \} = P \{\eta_ {n} > s _ {n} \}, \quad n = 1, \dots , k,
$$

so the random variables $\eta_{n}^{t}$ have the same distribution as $\eta_{n}$ . Inserting this back into the first equality, we obtain

$$
P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, \dots , \eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k} \right\} = P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k} \right\},
$$

so the $\eta_n^t$ are independent.

To prove that the $\eta_n^t$ are independent of $N(t)$ integrate the formula in Exercise 6.10 over $\{N(t) = n\}$ and multiply by $P\{N(t) = n\}$ to get

$$
P \left\{\eta_ {1} ^ {t} > s _ {1}, \dots , \eta_ {k} ^ {t} > s _ {k}, N (t) = n \right\} = P \left\{\eta_ {1} > s _ {1} \right\} \dots P \left\{\eta_ {k} > s _ {k} \right\} P \left\{N (t) = n \right\}.
$$

But $P\{\eta_n^t > s_n\} = P\{\eta_n > s_n\}$ , hence $N(t)$ and the $\eta_n^t$ are independent.

# Solution 6.12

We need to verify conditions 1), 2), 3) of Definition 6.3. Clearly, $N(t) - \lambda t$ is $\mathcal{F}_t$ -measurable. By Exercise 6.6

$$
E \left(| N (t) |\right) = E \left(N (t)\right) = \lambda t <   \infty ,
$$

which means that $N(t)$ is integrable, and so is $N(t) - \lambda t$ .

Theorem 6.2 implies that $N(t) - N(s)$ is independent of $\mathcal{F}_s$ for any $0 \leq s \leq t$ , so

$$
E (N (t) - N (s) | \mathcal {F} _ {s}) = E (N (t) - N (s)) = E (N (t)) - E (N (s)) = \lambda t - \lambda s.
$$

It follows that

$$
E (N (t) - \lambda t | \mathcal {F} _ {s}) = E (N (s) - \lambda s | \mathcal {F} _ {s}) = N (s) - \lambda s,
$$

completing the proof.

# Solution 6.13

Since $\eta_{n} = \xi_{n} - \xi_{n - 1}$ and $P\{\eta_n > 0\} = e^0 = 1,$

$$
P \{\xi_ {0} <   \xi_ {1} <   \xi_ {2} <   \dots \} = P \left(\bigcap_ {n = 1} ^ {\infty} \{\eta_ {n} > 0 \}\right) = 1.
$$

Here we have used the property that if $P(A_{n}) = 1$ for all $n = 1,2,\ldots$ , then $P\left(\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}A_{n}\right) = 1$ .

# Solution 6.14

Since

$$
\lim _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \xi_ {n} = \sum_ {n = 1} ^ {\infty} \eta_ {n},
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\{\lim _ {n \rightarrow \infty} \xi_ {n} <   \infty \right\} \subset \left\{\eta_ {1}, \eta_ {2}, \dots \text { is   a   bounded   sequence } \right\} \\ = \bigcup_ {m = 1} ^ {\infty} \bigcap_ {n = 1} ^ {\infty} \left\{\eta_ {n} \leq m \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us compute the probability of this event. Because $\bigcap_{n=1}^{N}\{\eta_n \leq m\}$ , $N = 1, 2, \ldots$ is a contracting sequence of events,

$$
P \left(\bigcap_ {n = 1} ^ {\infty} \{\eta_ {n} \leq m \}\right) = \lim _ {N \rightarrow \infty} P \left(\bigcap_ {n = 1} ^ {N} \{\eta_ {n} \leq m \}\right)
$$
